PaternPractices onBasicSentences 19-9
3a. Magbabaligyaq akhingutíayngaisdaq.
b. Maykugadlaqgutíayngaigbábaligyaqhaímu.
4a. Magqisturyaakuhingutíayha ímu.
b. Maykugadlaqgutíayngaigqíisturyahaímu.
5a. Magsúsumat kuntaqakhingutíayhaímu.
b. Maykugadlaqgutíayngaigsúsumathaímu.
6a. Nagqaandamkuntaqakhingutíaypárahaíyu.
b. Maykugadlaqgutíayngaigqáandam haíyu.
7a. Maghihimangraw gadlaakhingutíayhaímu.

XXF. Mga Pakiána
1. Anuanginsaka niAmuay?

hubáraitInglisngamga pulunghaWaray:

Verbbasesmayhave nouns formed fromthembyadding
afixeswhichareanalogoustotheactiveconjugation. Nouns
formed inthiswayrefertotheagentoftheactionorone
whodoestheactionregularly. These nouns have ashort
reduplication with (symbolized -1-).
Ifthesecond-to-
lastsylableoftheverbisshort, theagent nounhasa
longsecond-to-lastsylable. Ifthesecond tolastsylable
oftheverbislong,theagentnounhasashort-second-to-last
sylable. (Thisissymbolized 34Sec.3,14)

thatdoformnouns inthiswaychooseonlyoneofthethree

ofthestoryforLesson21. Thepeculiarities oftheNorthern
Samardialecthavebeeneditedout. Huwan Pusung 'Lazy,Lying
John'isthename giventotheprotagonistoffolkstories
aboutalazy,lyingpersonwho takesadvantageofsomeoneelse
andwho alsodoesfoolishthings. Storiesabout Huwan Púsung
arecurentthroughout thePhilippinesandmany ofthemprob-
ablyhavebeen handeddownfromgenerationtogenerationsince
pre-Spanish times.

XXIIE. FreeConversation
A. Pretend you've justarivedatyourfriend'shouse
inGuiuan. Mrs.Reyes, Arturo'smother,opens thedoorand
exclaims thatyou'reherealready. Yousayyes,andyou
explainyoudidnotbotherwaitingforArturotomeetyouat
theboatbecause youhadafriendattheboatwhotookyouto
thisplace. Mrs.Reyes saysthat'sverynice,andadds that

gomeetyouattheboat. Yousaythatnowyou'reherehewon't
have tobother. Mrs.Reyes askswhattimeyouarived. You
telheryouarivedatsixo'clock. Shetelsyouhowshe
askedamanattheoficewhattimetheboatwasarivingand
he saideighto'clock. Shesay'sthatmanwassuchaliar.

cheatedyou. Justbecauseyou'reanAmericanhere,heraised

Commentary totheReading
ThisstorywastoldbyMrs. MilagrosTagros, born1922
inBobon,Samar. Dialectalpeculiarities havebeen removed.
Sandapalisfromthewordsusangadapal'
onehand's breadth.
InotherversionsofthestorySandapaliscaledDangáway
(fromdángaw,thelengthbetween thetipofthethumbtothe
tipofthethirdfinger) orKarangkál (fromusakadangkál
[theTagalogwordfordángaw]). Apointofalthevarious
versionofthisstoryisthatSandapalwasverysmalbut
hadextraordinarystrength.

23.92 Masvadu'verymuch'
Masvádu isasynonymofdúruand occursinthesame
grammatical constructionasdúru(Sec.22.311). Mostcommonly
itoccursintheconstructionmasyáduhinka-[adjective]'very
[adjective]':
Masyádu hinkahubyaq hanbátaq. The childwasvery
lazy.
Masyádu anbátaqhinkahubyaq.
Thechildwasvery
lazy.
'
Thehinmaybedropped iftheadjectivemodifiesaverb:
Iningabátaqmasyádu kamakusugmagtrabáhu.
Thischildworked veryhard.
'

uraúra:
Iningabátaquraúra hinkahubyaq.

UsakaBatásanhinPangasawa
1. UsangakinaiyahanmaPilipinunga itunlalákiámu
annásakahinpagpangasawa. Dámuq anmagkalainláinnga
kustumri ngaginbúbúhat bahinhanpagpangasawa. Usahini
ámu anpaggamit hansinisiringngaismayling.
Ininga
ismaylingkinadaan iningapaágipagsáritpangasawa, nga
ginágámit padidtuhamgairayahaSámar.
2. Kunanusaka ialákikarúyagmangasáwa hindarága,
nagdádápit hiyahinusangatinahud ngatawu ha bungtu,ug
upudhitíyamga kagqanak, nákadtuhirahabalayhanbabáyi
pakigqatúbang hituniyamgakagqanak. Nagdádara hirahin
pagkẩun nganhintubaqngairimnun,ngandidaqhininga
harampang ginkåkanta han tináhud nga lalakianismayling,
nganbinábatun man liwathinusangatigúrangngababáyi
ngadinápitmanhankagganak handarágangapinanngángasawa.
Didaqhiningabatunbátun iginpapahayag hitunnalalalakihan
ituníratúyuq,ngansúgad man, annabábabayíhannagpápaháyag
manhaníradispusisyunpinaágihituníramga batun.
3.Dámuq itmagkalainláinngatúnuhitunismayting.Aadaq
laqitunhitmagkaranta itpagpíliqhittúm ngaíragága-
mítun, Usáhay ngániq, nagtútúhaqhiradidaqmantaqhitun
ngaharampang hinírakalugaríngunngatúnu. Kunanu nga
túnuitgamítunhitnagtitikang pagkanta, ámuman itgágamítun
nirangaduha hastahitkatapusan*
. Itunmgapúlungnga

CommentarytotheReading
ThisstorywastoldbyMarciano Caparoso,whointroduces
himselfinthefirstparagraph. Thepeculiarities ofthe
Palapagdialecthavebeenremoved.
AdefinitivehistoryoftheSumuroy rebelionstil
remains tobewriten, andwhatistoldheremustbetaken
withagrainofsalt. The rebelionoccured intheyears
1649-1650 (not1849asthenaratortels) anditspreadfrom
Samartoseveralotherlocations inthePhilippines. The
ause oftherebelionwastheimpressmentoflabortoCavite
toworkontheManilaGaleon(inbuildingtheshipsandper-
hapsevenmanning them). Afteraltheevidencehasbeen
examined,itwilprobably turnoutthatSumuroywasfrom
Palapag,and thatthefinalbatlewasfought inKasilias
Palapagtraditionhasit.
Thereisanother unsubstantiated
traditionthatSumuroywas fromLeyte. Itisnotunlikely
thataSpanish priesttoldaboutherewaskiledduringthis

rebelion. Whetherthepriest'snamewasJuanPoncehasnot
been establishedwithcertainty.

saysSumuroy'snamewasJuanFonce. Asfarasthetradition
ofSumuroy'svisittoKabatuan isconcerned,thepartabout
therockwhichwasbrought toPalapagisofcourseimpossible,
butitisnotunlikelythatwhenSumuroyhidfromtheSpaniards
hewasforsome timeatKabatuan.

XXVK. Pagsurat. Pagqadmi itmásunudngamgapúlungngan
hubárahaWarayitInglisngamga púlung:
1. AnláwasniSumúduy paríhuhaátun,díriqman
higanti.
Sumurey'sstrengthwasnotlikethatofa
giant's,forhewasonlyanordinaryperson.
2. Maúpayhiyamagdara hanmga táwu.
HewasnotsogoodatobeyingtheSpaniards.
3. Tungud haníyakamaúpaymagdara,dámuq haíyaan
binunyugpagkuntrahanmgaKatsilaq.
Becausehewasafastrunner,many Spaniards
wanted himtofolowthem.
4.

Para- isadded toverbstoformnouns whichmean
'onewhousualydoes [verb]. Whenpara-( )isadded toa
root,thesecond-to-last-sylableoftherootislongwhenthe
rootwithoutpara-isshort; whenthesecond-to-lastsylable

withpara-isshort: paraárut'barber,' paralába'laundryman,
laundress,
'paramasahi'masseur,' parasuguq 'mayordomo[one
whogivesorders],
'parakanhi'onewhoalwayscomeshere.

27.61 Theappositivegenitive construction
Agenitivewhichexplainsthenounitmodifiesiscaled
anAPPOSITIVEGENITIVE, e.g. syudad ha Taklúban'cityof
Tacloban,
'gubyirnu haIspanya'thegovernment ofSpain,
'
islahaHumunhun'islandofHomonhon, etc. Haisused
insteadofhan, hittomarktheappositivegenitivebefore

namesofplacesandothernounwithwhichhacommonlyisused
insteadofhan,hit(see thediscussioninSec. 11.33). This
useofhaisoptional: hitandhanmayalsomarktheapposi-
tivegenitive construction: gubvirnu hanPilipinas'the
government ofthePhilippines.
'
27.62 Ha toreplacehit, han, hin
Before nouns haoptionalymay replace hit, hanorhinto
express location(expressthemeaning'at, in, on'). (With
somewords[namesofplacesandothers]haisalwaysused to
mean lat,in, on, etc. see thediscussioninSec.11.33.)-
Withmost nounshaoroptionalyhit, han,hinexpresses loca-
tion.
Nakadaq anmgaPuláhanha(orhan)maybukid.
'

'The

waswelknownnotonlyhereinPalapag, butalsoin
thenearbytowns.
'
Withthewordsdescribed inSec. 11.33haisalwaysused
toexpress location ('at, on, in, etc.)neverhit,han,hin:
DidtuhiyahaPalápag.
'He wasatPalapag.

conjugation Sec.8.2)arecaledSTATIVEVERBS.
28.11 The localpassive ofstative verbs
Moststativeverbshave alocalpassive potentialconju-
gation. Theactivereferstoafocuswhichisthethingor
personwhichcame into[such-and-such]astate: e.g.,the
personorthingwho fel(nahúlug), thepersonorthingwho
died(namatay), theperson orthingwhichdisappeared (nawa-
raq),etc. Thelocalpassivereferstoapersonorthingto
whomithappened thatsomething fel, died, disappeared, etc.
Inthefolowing pairedexamples theverbisunderlinedtwice
andthewordinfocus, once. Thefirstofthesepairs
ilustratestheactive;thesecondthelocalpassive:

phrases,anyWarayformhasanumber ofEnglishequivalents.

simply'arive.

[verb]ed' (e.g.ginkáun'thingeaten'); localpassivesare

of[verb]ing' dependingonthemeaning appropriateforthe
root; andablative passivesareglossed'thingwithwhich
[verb]ed,
' 'thing[verb]ed,
' or'timeof[verb]ing,

isappropriate.
